=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        toppingCosts *= 2
        
        @cache
        def fn(i, x):
            """Return sum of subsequence of toppingCosts[i:] closest to x."""
            if x < 0 or i = len(toppingCosts): return 0
            return min(fn(i+1, x), toppingCosts[i] + fn(i+1, x-toppingCosts[i]), key=lambda y: (abs(y-x), y))
        
        ans = infinity
        for bc in baseCosts: 
            ans = min(ans, bc + fn(0, target - bc), key=lambda x: (abs(x-target), x))
        return ans

=== PROMPT ===
Observe the following faulty python code. It contains one or more bugs.
Fix the code and output only the fixed code.

```python
class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        toppingCosts *= 2
        
        @cache
        def fn(i, x):
            """Return sum of subsequence of toppingCosts[i:] closest to x."""
            if x < 0 or i = len(toppingCosts): return 0
            return min(fn(i+1, x), toppingCosts[i] + fn(i+1, x-toppingCosts[i]), key=lambda y: (abs(y-x), y))
        
        ans = infinity
        for bc in baseCosts: 
            ans = min(ans, bc + fn(0, target - bc), key=lambda x: (abs(x-target), x))
        return ans
```

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Output only the fixed Python code.

But let's think about a possible flaw in the recurrence due to `fn` returning 0 for x<0 and using it in take branch. We considered. Let's formalize: Let T(i) = toppingCosts[i:]. Define F(i,x) = argmin_{S subset of T(i)} |sum(S)-x|, tie lower sum. For x<0, since all sums >=0, F(i,x)=0. For i==len, F=0. For i<len and x>=0, recurrence: F(i,x) = min(F(i+1,x), toppingCosts[i] + F(i+1, x-toppingCosts[i])). Is this valid for x>=0? Need check if x-toppingCosts[i] can be negative. If x-toppingCosts[i] <0, F(i+1, negative)=0, so take branch = toppingCosts[i]. That means among subsets including i, the best is to take i and no further toppings. Is that always true? We need minimize |toppingCosts[i] + S' - x| for S' subset of T(i+1). Let y = x - toppingCosts[i] <0. Then |toppingCosts[i]+S'-x| = |S' - y|. Since y<0, all S'>=0, the closest to y is 0. So yes. Need decide exact code. Let's craft:

```python
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        toppingCosts *= 2

        @lru_cache(None)
        def fn(i: int, x: int) -> int:
            if x < 0 or i == len(toppingCosts):
                return 0
            skip = fn(i + 1, x)
            take = toppingCosts[i] + fn(i + 1, x - toppingCosts[i])
            return min(skip, take, key=lambda y: (abs(y - x), y))

        ans = float('inf')
        for bc in baseCosts:
            ans = min(ans, bc + fn(0, target - bc), key=lambda cost: (abs(cost - target), cost))
        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        toppingCosts *= 2

        @lru_cache(None)
        def fn(i: int, x: int) -> int:
            if x < 0 or i == len(toppingCosts):
                return 0
            skip = fn(i + 1, x)
            take = toppingCosts[i] + fn(i + 1, x - toppingCosts[i])
            return min(skip, take, key=lambda y: (abs(y - x), y))

        ans = float('inf')
        for bc in baseCosts:
            ans = min(ans, bc + fn(0, target - bc), key=lambda cost: (abs(cost - target), cost))
        return ans
